# CELL 0 markdown
# Gemma 4 Developer Agent: the audit and power tables, regenerated on CPU

Team nitish5236goel. Companion to our Writeup for the Gemma 4 Developer Agent paper track.

The competition's materials exist in two releases, and the Writeup audits the first:

* **As first published**: the competition data of 22 September 2026 and the harness wheels as
  downloaded on 24 September, which participants used until 25 September.
* **As re-issued on 25 September 2026**: Kaggle re-uploaded the data and the harness wheels under
  the same version numbers. `tasks.jsonl` and the 124 dependency wheels are byte-identical and the
  snapshots have the same sizes. The task-named graph and embedding files are gone, so each
  commit keeps one non-empty graph and one non-empty embedding file, each of the size its
  commit's non-empty copy had before.

This notebook regenerates the tables the Writeup quotes, for the materials as first published:

* the per-task audit of the 129 practice tasks (`tasks_audit.csv`, `summary.json`, `summary.md`);
* the graph audit: which graph and embedding files the harness reads, and what the graphs contain;
* the resolution (power) tables (`power_table.csv`, `.json`, `.md`).

Section 10 then measures the re-issue on the attached files and compares it with the first
release. The notebook needs no GPU, no internet and no competition submission. At the end it
compares every regenerated table with the copy committed in the team's repository (commit
`7a286d8a72a6`) and lists each figure the Writeup quotes beside its regenerated value.

| Input | Where it comes from here |
|---|---|
| Sizes of the graph and embedding files, as first published | the file listing recorded through the Kaggle API on 24 September 2026; the attached files are compared with it |
| Sizes of the graph and embedding files, as re-issued | measured on the attached files (the listing recorded on 26 September when the attached files are not the re-issue) |
| Functions the reference patches change | recomputed from the snapshot archives, if attached |
| Graph content: nodes, `async` definitions, coverage of the changed functions | recomputed from the attached graph files |
| Official-verifier outcomes (1,210 runs in 6 environments, and 32 runs repeated on the re-issued data) | recorded: each run builds a sandbox and runs pytest; the first 1,210 took 5.4 hours of wall time in all |
| What the graph tools return when called through the harness, for both releases | recorded: this needs the harness package, which is not part of the competition data |

When an input is not attached, the step uses the recorded measurement and says so. Recorded
measurements are embedded below with SHA-256 checksums. Nothing from the competition data is
copied into the outputs or printed except derived measurements: counts, sizes, dates, timings,
identifiers, and the names of changed files and functions. No problem statement, patch or
snapshot content.

Code and text by the team are released under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (Apache-2.0).

# CELL 2 markdown
## 1. Find the competition data

The path is resolved at run time, never assumed. The resolver tries the two layouts Kaggle has
used for attached competition data in the team's other kernels, then searches below the input root (breadth first,
at most three levels and 400 directories), then the repository's `data/` directory above the
starting directory, for runs off Kaggle. `G4_DATA_DIR` overrides the search.

# CELL 3 code
COMPETITION = "gemma-4-developer-agent"
MARKER = "tasks.jsonl"
ENV_OVERRIDE = "G4_DATA_DIR"
INPUT_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("G4_INPUT_ROOT", "/kaggle/input"))
MAX_DEPTH, MAX_DIRS = 3, 400


def discover(root: Path) -> list:
    # directories at most MAX_DEPTH levels below root that contain MARKER; bounded, no glob
    hits, queue, visited = [], [(root, 0)], 0
    while queue and visited < MAX_DIRS:
        d, depth = queue.pop(0)
        visited += 1
        try:
            entries = sorted(os.scandir(d), key=lambda e: e.name)
            if any(e.name == MARKER and e.is_file() for e in entries):
                hits.append(Path(d))
                continue
            if depth < MAX_DEPTH:
                queue += [(Path(e.path), depth + 1) for e in entries if e.is_dir()]
        except OSError:
            continue
    return hits


def candidate_dirs() -> list:
    out = [INPUT_ROOT / "competitions" / COMPETITION, INPUT_ROOT / COMPETITION]
    if INPUT_ROOT.is_dir():
        out += discover(INPUT_ROOT)
    out += [p / "data" / COMPETITION for p in [START_DIR, *START_DIR.parents][:6]]
    seen, uniq = set(), []
    for p in out:
        if str(p) not in seen:
            seen.add(str(p))
            uniq.append(p)
    return uniq


def describe_mounts(root: Path, max_lines: int = 30) -> str:
    if not root.is_dir():
        return f"  {root} does not exist (nothing is attached)"
    lines = []
    for e in sorted(os.scandir(root), key=lambda e: e.name)[:max_lines]:
        try:
            sub = sorted(x.name for x in os.scandir(e.path))[:12] if e.is_dir() else []
        except OSError as err:
            sub = [f"unreadable: {type(err).__name__}"]
        lines.append(f"  {e.path}  {sub}")
    return "\n".join(lines) or f"  {root} is empty"


def find_data_dir() -> Path:
    override = os.environ.get(ENV_OVERRIDE)
    cands = [Path(override)] if override else candidate_dirs()
    for d in cands:
        if (d / MARKER).is_file():
            return d.resolve()
    tried = "\n".join(f"  {d}" for d in cands)
    raise FileNotFoundError(
        f"Competition data not found: no {MARKER} in any candidate directory.\nTried:\n{tried}\n"
        f"Mounted under {INPUT_ROOT}:\n{describe_mounts(INPUT_ROOT)}\n"
        f"Set {ENV_OVERRIDE} to the directory that holds {MARKER}, or attach the competition data "
        f'("competition_sources": ["{COMPETITION}"] in kernel-metadata.json).')


DATA = find_data_dir()
print(f"Competition data: {DATA}")
for sub in ("graphs", "embeddings", "snapshots"):
    d = DATA / sub
    print(f"  {sub}/: " + (f"{sum(1 for e in os.scandir(d) if e.is_file())} files" if d.is_dir() else "not attached"))

%%writefile g4src/g4bench/__init__.py
"""Measurement code for the Gemma 4 Developer Agent benchmark resource paper.

Everything in this package is pure Python and runs on CPU. Modules that need the
official harness (`swegemma`) import it lazily, so the package and its tests import
without it.
"""

# CELL 6 code
%%writefile g4src/g4bench/budget.py
"""What the host's sample `eval_config.yaml` would do to an agent, derived from swegemma 0.2.7.

The sample file:

    evaluation:
      timeout_seconds: 60
      max_tool_calls: 10
      max_time_minutes: 1
      max_turns: 50

The four keys are exactly the flat keyword arguments of `swegemma.config.EvalConfig`
(`timeout_seconds`, `max_tool_calls`, `max_time_minutes`, `max_turns`), which is the
natural way for a wrapper to apply them. The wrapper that reads the file was not
published, so *whether* and *where* it applies them is an assumption; everything below
is conditional on "applied as EvalConfig keyword arguments", and each consequence is
read off the shipped code:

* `max_time_minutes: 1` -> `budget.time_minutes = 1`. The agent loop runs inside
  `asyncio.timeout(60)`; generation is cut mid-turn when the minute is up.
* `max_tool_calls: 10` -> `budget.tool_calls = 10`. The 11th budget-gated call returns
  `BudgetExceeded`. `submit_patch` and `get_status` are not counted.
* The low-budget warning (`budget_warning` field) is attached only when
  `budget.tool_calls >= 20`, so with 10 the agent is never warned.
* `max_turns: 50` -> `budget.turns = 50` (loose next to the other two).
* `timeout_seconds: 60` -> `harness.command_timeout_seconds = 60`. This is also the
  sandbox manager's default timeout for *every* command without an explicit one,
  which includes all container-setup commands (snapshot extraction, editable install,
  test-dependency setup), and it is the timeout of the verification pytest run in
  Container B if the same configuration reaches Stage 2.
* `run_command` timeout becomes `min(60, max(5, remaining seconds))`.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass

SAMPLE_EVAL_CONFIG = {"timeout_seconds": 60, "max_tool_calls": 10, "max_time_minutes": 1, "max_turns": 50}
HARNESS_DEFAULTS = {"timeout_seconds": 300, "max_tool_calls": None, "max_time_minutes": 60.0, "max_turns": 500}
WARNING_MIN_BUDGET = 20  # swegemma.tools.base._attach_low_budget_warning: max_calls < 20 -> no warning
WARNING_REMAINING = 10


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Effective:
    time_minutes: float | None
    tool_calls: int | None
    turns: int | None
    command_timeout_seconds: int | None


def effective(cfg: dict) -> Effective:
    """Budget after EvalConfig.__post_init__ merges the flat keys over the defaults."""
    return Effective(
        time_minutes=cfg.get("max_time_minutes", HARNESS_DEFAULTS["max_time_minutes"]),
        tool_calls=cfg.get("max_tool_calls", HARNESS_DEFAULTS["max_tool_calls"]),
        turns=cfg.get("max_turns", HARNESS_DEFAULTS["max_turns"]),
        command_timeout_seconds=cfg.get("timeout_seconds", HARNESS_DEFAULTS["timeout_seconds"]),
    )


def warning_ever_shown(tool_calls: int | None) -> bool:
    """Whether any tool response can carry `budget_warning` under this tool-call budget."""
    return tool_calls is not None and tool_calls >= WARNING_MIN_BUDGET


def warning_first_at(tool_calls: int | None) -> int | None:
    """1-based index of the first call whose response carries the warning, or None."""
    if not warning_ever_shown(tool_calls):
        return None
    # the warning is attached when remaining (max - used, counted after the call) <= 10
    return tool_calls - WARNING_REMAINING


def run_command_timeout(command_timeout: int, remaining_seconds: float) -> int:
    """swegemma run_command: min(command_timeout, max(5, int(remaining)))."""
    return min(command_timeout, max(5, int(remaining_seconds)))


def budget_block(e: Effective) -> list[str]:
    """The lines the harness prints under '## Task Budget' (agent_runner.build_agent_prompt)."""
    out = []
    if e.time_minutes is not None:
        out.append(f"- Time allowance: {e.time_minutes} minutes")
    if e.tool_calls is not None:
        out.append(f"- Tool calls allowance: {e.tool_calls} calls")
    if e.turns is not None:
        out.append(f"- Max loop iterations: {e.turns} turns")
    return out


def full_thinking_turn_seconds(thinking_tokens: int, decode_tokens_per_s: float) -> float:
    """Wall time of one turn that uses its whole thinking budget, at a given decode rate.

    The decode rate of the scorer's 4 x L4 deployment is not published and was not
    measured here; this is arithmetic for a stated rate, not a measurement.
    """
    return thinking_tokens / decode_tokens_per_s


def break_even_decode_rate(thinking_tokens: int, seconds: float) -> float:
    """Decode rate below which one full-thinking turn alone exceeds `seconds`."""
    return thinking_tokens / seconds


def rough_tokens(chars: int, chars_per_token: float = 3.5) -> int:
    """A labelled approximation: no Gemma tokenizer is used offline here."""
    return int(round(chars / chars_per_token))

# CELL 7 code
%%writefile g4src/g4bench/diffs.py
"""Unified-diff parsing for the task patches.

The task patches in `tasks.jsonl` are plain `---`/`+++` diffs without `diff --git`
headers. A file created by the patch is written as `--- a/<path>` with a single
`@@ -0,0 +1,N @@` hunk rather than against `/dev/null`, so creation is detected
from the hunk ranges, not from the header.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

_HUNK_RE = re.compile(r"^@@ -(\d+)(?:,(\d+))? \+(\d+)(?:,(\d+))? @@ ?(.*)$")
_DEF_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*(async\s+)?def\s+([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)")
_CLASS_RE = re.compile(r"^\s*class\s+([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*)")


@dataclass
class Hunk:
    old_start: int
    old_len: int
    new_start: int
    new_len: int
    context: str  # git's function-context text after the second @@, possibly ''
    lines: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)  # raw body lines incl. prefix

    @property
    def added(self) -> list[str]:
        return [ln[1:] for ln in self.lines if ln.startswith("+")]

    @property
    def removed(self) -> list[str]:
        return [ln[1:] for ln in self.lines if ln.startswith("-")]

    def changed_old_lines(self) -> list[int]:
        """Old-file line numbers that are removed, or that an insertion follows."""
        out: list[int] = []
        old = self.old_start
        prev_old = self.old_start - 1
        for ln in self.lines:
            if ln.startswith("-"):
                out.append(old)
                prev_old = old
                old += 1
            elif ln.startswith("+"):
                # a pure insertion sits after prev_old; attribute it to that line
                out.append(max(prev_old, 1) if self.old_len else 0)
            elif ln.startswith(" ") or ln == "":
                prev_old = old
                old += 1
        return sorted(set(x for x in out if x > 0))


@dataclass
class FileDiff:
    old_path: str
    new_path: str
    hunks: list[Hunk] = field(default_factory=list)

    @property
    def path(self) -> str:
        return self.new_path if self.new_path != "/dev/null" else self.old_path

    @property
    def is_new(self) -> bool:
        if self.old_path == "/dev/null":
            return True
        return len(self.hunks) == 1 and self.hunks[0].old_start == 0 and self.hunks[0].old_len == 0

    @property
    def is_deleted(self) -> bool:
        if self.new_path == "/dev/null":
            return True
        return len(self.hunks) == 1 and self.hunks[0].new_start == 0 and self.hunks[0].new_len == 0

    @property
    def added(self) -> int:
        return sum(len(h.added) for h in self.hunks)

    @property
    def removed(self) -> int:
        return sum(len(h.removed) for h in self.hunks)


def _strip_prefix(p: str) -> str:
    p = p.strip().split("\t", 1)[0].strip('"')
    if p.startswith(("a/", "b/")):
        return p[2:]
    return p


def parse_diff(text: str) -> list[FileDiff]:
    """Parse a unified diff into files and hunks. Tolerates `diff --git` headers."""
    files: list[FileDiff] = []
    cur: FileDiff | None = None
    hunk: Hunk | None = None
    lines = text.splitlines()
    i = 0
    while i < len(lines):
        ln = lines[i]
        if ln.startswith("--- ") and i + 1 < len(lines) and lines[i + 1].startswith("+++ "):
            cur = FileDiff(_strip_prefix(ln[4:]), _strip_prefix(lines[i + 1][4:]))
            files.append(cur)
            hunk = None
            i += 2
            continue
        m = _HUNK_RE.match(ln)
        if m and cur is not None:
            hunk = Hunk(
                old_start=int(m.group(1)),
                old_len=int(m.group(2)) if m.group(2) is not None else 1,
                new_start=int(m.group(3)),
                new_len=int(m.group(4)) if m.group(4) is not None else 1,
                context=m.group(5) or "",
            )
            cur.hunks.append(hunk)
            i += 1
            continue
        if hunk is not None and (ln[:1] in ("+", "-", " ") or ln == "") and not ln.startswith("diff --git"):
            hunk.lines.append(ln)
        elif ln.startswith("\\"):
            pass  # "\ No newline at end of file"
        i += 1
    return files


def is_test_path(path: str) -> bool:
    """A path the curation pipeline would call a test file (`test_*.py`, `*_test.py`, or under tests/)."""
    name = path.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
    if name.startswith("test_") and name.endswith(".py"):
        return True
    if name.endswith("_test.py") or name == "conftest.py":
        return True
    parts = [p.lower() for p in path.split("/")[:-1]]
    return any(p in ("tests", "test", "testing") for p in parts) and name.endswith(".py")


def hunk_context_symbols(files: list[FileDiff]) -> list[tuple[str, str, bool]]:
    """(path, symbol, is_async) for every def/class named in a hunk's git function context.

    Git's default Python funcname heuristic names the nearest preceding `def` or
    `class` line. It is a proxy for the enclosing symbol: it names the line above the
    hunk, which for a hunk that starts between two functions is the earlier one.
    """
    out = []
    for f in files:
        for h in f.hunks:
            m = _DEF_RE.match(h.context)
            if m:
                out.append((f.path, m.group(2), bool(m.group(1))))
                continue
            m = _CLASS_RE.match(h.context)
            if m:
                out.append((f.path, m.group(1), False))
    return out


def defined_symbols(lines: list[str]) -> list[tuple[str, bool]]:
    """(name, is_async) for every def in the given source lines."""
    out = []
    for ln in lines:
        m = _DEF_RE.match(ln)
        if m:
            out.append((m.group(2), bool(m.group(1))))
    return out

# CELL 8 code
%%writefile g4src/g4bench/graphs.py
"""Which code-graph files the harness reads for a task, and what it does when they are empty.

Mirrors swegemma 0.2.7:

* `harness/agent_runner.build_agent_prompt` appends the "Code Intelligence Tools"
  block only if some graph candidate AND some embedding candidate exist with size
  > 100 bytes. Candidates, in order: `<repo_short>_<base_commit>`,
  `<owner>_<repo>_<base_commit>`, `<owner>_<repo>`, `<repo_short>` (+ `.json` / `.npz`).
* `graph/graph_utils.get_graph` (behind all three graph tools) opens the *first
  candidate that exists*, empty or not. An empty file therefore fails `json.load`,
  and every graph tool returns an error instead of falling through to a later name.

The task-named files (`<instance_id>.json`, `.npz`) are never among the candidates.
The competition ships each graph and embedding twice, task-named and commit-named,
as hard links; on Kaggle one side of every pair is 0 bytes. Whether a task keeps its
graph tools therefore depends on which side survived.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path

GATE_BYTES = 100


def load_listing(path: Path) -> dict[str, int]:
    """{competition file name: size in bytes} from a saved Kaggle file listing."""
    rows = json.loads(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    return {r["name"]: int(r["size"]) for r in rows}


def task_file_names(task: dict) -> dict[str, str]:
    short = task["repo"].split("/")[-1]
    return {
        "graph_commit": f"graphs/{short}_{task['base_commit']}.json",
        "graph_task": f"graphs/{task['instance_id']}.json",
        "embedding_commit": f"embeddings/{short}_{task['base_commit']}.npz",
        "embedding_task": f"embeddings/{task['instance_id']}.npz",
    }


def _candidates(task: dict, kind: str) -> list[str]:
    repo = task["repo"]
    short = repo.split("/")[-1]
    slug = repo.replace("/", "_")
    d, ext = ("graphs", ".json") if kind == "graph" else ("embeddings", ".npz")
    c = [f"{d}/{short}_{task['base_commit']}{ext}", f"{d}/{slug}_{task['base_commit']}{ext}"]
    c += [f"{d}/{slug}{ext}", f"{d}/{short}{ext}"]
    return c


def prompt_block_shown(task: dict, sizes: dict[str, int]) -> bool:
    """The harness's gate: some graph candidate and some embedding candidate > 100 bytes."""
    has_g = any(sizes.get(p, -1) > GATE_BYTES for p in _candidates(task, "graph"))
    has_e = any(sizes.get(p, -1) > GATE_BYTES for p in _candidates(task, "embedding"))
    return has_g and has_e


def first_existing(task: dict, kind: str, sizes: dict[str, int]) -> str | None:
    for p in _candidates(task, kind):
        if p in sizes:
            return p
    return None


def classify(task: dict, sizes: dict[str, int]) -> dict:
    """Per-task status of the graph data as the harness sees it."""
    names = task_file_names(task)
    s = {k: sizes.get(v) for k, v in names.items()}
    g_first = first_existing(task, "graph", sizes)
    e_first = first_existing(task, "embedding", sizes)
    g_ok = g_first is not None and sizes[g_first] > GATE_BYTES
    e_ok = e_first is not None and sizes[e_first] > GATE_BYTES
    block = prompt_block_shown(task, sizes)
    if g_ok and e_ok:
        cls = "A_intact"
    elif g_ok:
        cls = "B_graph_only"
    elif e_ok:
        cls = "C_embeddings_only"
    else:
        cls = "D_neither"
    content_somewhere = {
        "graph": any((sizes.get(names[k]) or 0) > GATE_BYTES for k in ("graph_commit", "graph_task")),
        "embedding": any((sizes.get(names[k]) or 0) > GATE_BYTES for k in ("embedding_commit", "embedding_task")),
    }
    return {
        "sizes": s,
        "graph_read": g_first,
        "embedding_read": e_first,
        "graph_read_nonempty": g_ok,
        "embedding_read_nonempty": e_ok,
        "prompt_block": block,
        "class": cls,
        "graph_content_under_task_name_only": (not g_ok) and (s["graph_task"] or 0) > GATE_BYTES,
        "embedding_content_under_task_name_only": (not e_ok) and (s["embedding_task"] or 0) > GATE_BYTES,
        "content_exists": content_somewhere,
    }

# CELL 9 code
%%writefile g4src/g4bench/harness_env.py
"""Run the official swegemma verifier on a machine without Docker.

The harness has two sandbox backends. `ContainerManager` (Docker) is what the host
describes as the default; `SubprocessManager` (a directory plus a virtualenv) is the
one that runs here. They are not equivalent for verification: the harness branches on
the sandbox's class name, and for a class named `SubprocessManager` it skips test
dependency installation entirely (`install_test_dependencies` returns at once), relying
on whatever the host Python already has.

To follow the Docker code path on a Docker-less machine, `make_manager` builds a
subclass of `SubprocessManager` that

* is named `ContainerManager`, so every `type(docker).__name__` branch in the harness
  takes its Docker arm (dependency injection runs; the JUnit report is required);
* implements `extract_archive_to_container`, the method the Docker path uses to unpack
  the snapshot and to stream unpacked wheels into site-packages;
* maps the Docker image's absolute paths (`/usr/local/lib/python3.13/site-packages`,
  `/var/tmp`) into the sandbox, which the subprocess backend does not translate and
  which would otherwise touch the host;
* emulates the image: the sandbox virtualenv sees exactly one extra site-packages
  directory, a virtualenv built from `Dockerfile.sandbox`'s pip line (pytest,
  pytest-timeout 2.1.0, the five build backends) plus the image's `imp.py` and
  `telnetlib.py` shims, and `/wheels` holds the competition's 124 wheels, as the
  read-only mount does;
* records every command with its wall time, exit status and timeout.

Known differences from the Docker image, stated so they are not mistaken for
equivalence: the subprocess backend also sets `PYTHONPATH=/workspace`; there is no
2-vCPU / 4 GiB limit and no network isolation; and the image's packages sit in a
separate directory on `sys.path` rather than in the interpreter's own site-packages.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import types
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

IMAGE_SITE_PACKAGES = "/usr/local/lib/python3.13/site-packages"


def translate_image_paths(command: str, site_packages: str, var_tmp: str) -> str:
    """Rewrite the Docker image's absolute paths to their sandbox locations."""
    command = command.replace(IMAGE_SITE_PACKAGES, site_packages)
    return re.sub(r"(?<![\w.-])/var/tmp(?![\w.-])", var_tmp, command)


def classify_command(command: str) -> str:
    """A short label for a sandbox command, for timing tables."""
    c = command
    if "-m pytest" in c and "--junitxml" in c:
        return "pytest_verify"
    if "tar " in c and ("-C " in c):
        return "untar"
    if "pip install" in c and "-e " in c:
        return "editable_install"
    if "setup.py" in c and "--fast-path" in c:
        return "test_deps_setup"
    if "apply_patch" in c or "git apply" in c:
        return "apply_patch"
    if 'git commit -m "eval_baseline"' in c or "git commit -m \"baseline\"" in c:
        return "baseline_commit"
    if "git checkout HEAD --" in c or "git clean -f --" in c:
        return "reset_tests"
    if "import pathlib, re; mods" in c:
        return "import_scan"
    return "other"


def origin_probe(package: str) -> str:
    """A command that prints where `package` is imported from, with pytest's precedence.

    pytest (default "prepend" import mode) puts the rootdir of /workspace/conftest.py at
    the front of sys.path, so the probe does the same before importing.
    """
    code = (
        "import sys; sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace'); "
        f"import {package} as m; print('ORIGIN', getattr(m, '__file__', None))"
    )
    return f'cd /workspace && PYTHONSAFEPATH=1 PYTHONNOUSERSITE=1 python3 -s -c "{code}"'


def stub_model_serving() -> None:
    """Replace `google.adk.models.lite_llm` with a stub before swegemma is imported.

    swegemma's model registry imports LiteLLM at module import time. Verification
    never serves a model, and installing LiteLLM costs disk this machine does not have.
    The stub raises if anything tries to construct a model through it.
    """
    if "google.adk.models.lite_llm" in sys.modules:
        return
    mod = types.ModuleType("google.adk.models.lite_llm")

    class LiteLlm:  # noqa: D401 - stub
        def __init__(self, *a: Any, **k: Any) -> None:
            raise RuntimeError("model serving is stubbed out in CPU verification")

    mod.LiteLlm = LiteLlm
    sys.modules["google.adk.models.lite_llm"] = mod


def make_manager(
    *,
    image_site_packages: Path,
    wheels_dir: Path,
    base_dir: Path,
    timeout_seconds: int = 300,
    probe_command: str | None = None,
) -> Any:
    """Build the Docker-path emulating sandbox manager. Requires swegemma importable."""
    stub_model_serving()
    from swegemma.sandbox import SubprocessManager

    class ContainerManager(SubprocessManager):  # the name is load-bearing, see module doc
        def __init__(self) -> None:
            super().__init__(timeout_seconds=timeout_seconds, base_dir=base_dir, system_site_packages=False)
            self.command_log: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
            self.last_pytest: Any = None
            self.last_junit: str | None = None
            self.probe_command = probe_command
            self.probe_output: str | None = None

        def stop(self, sandbox_id: str) -> None:
            # run the probe in the verification sandbox just before it is torn down
            if self.probe_command and self.probe_output is None and sandbox_id in self._sandboxes:
                try:
                    r = super().exec(sandbox_id, self.probe_command, timeout=60)
                    self.probe_output = ((r.stdout or "") + (r.stderr or ""))[-400:]
                except Exception as e:  # the probe must never change the outcome
                    self.probe_output = f"probe failed: {e}"
            super().stop(sandbox_id)

        def _paths(self, sid: str) -> dict[str, Path]:
            return dict(self._sandboxes[sid])

        def _site_packages(self, sid: str) -> Path:
            return next(self._paths(sid)["venv"].glob("lib/python*/site-packages"))

        def start(self) -> str:
            sid = super().start()
            p = self._paths(sid)
            sp = self._site_packages(sid)
            (sp / "_host_env.pth").write_text(str(image_site_packages) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
            for w in sorted(wheels_dir.glob("*.whl")):
                (p["wheels"] / w.name).symlink_to(w.resolve())
            (p["root"] / "var" / "tmp").mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            return sid

        def _map(self, sid: str, command: str) -> str:
            p = self._paths(sid)
            return translate_image_paths(command, str(self._site_packages(sid)), str(p["root"] / "var" / "tmp"))

        def exec(self, sandbox_id: str, command: str, *, timeout: int | None = None) -> Any:
            mapped = self._map(sandbox_id, command)
            t0 = time.perf_counter()
            res = super().exec(sandbox_id, mapped, timeout=timeout)
            dt = time.perf_counter() - t0
            label = classify_command(command)
            self.command_log.append(
                {
                    "step": label,
                    "seconds": round(dt, 3),
                    "status": res.status,
                    "exit": res.exit_code,
                    "timeout": timeout if timeout is not None else self.timeout_seconds,
                }
            )
            if label == "pytest_verify":
                self.last_pytest = res
            if command.startswith("cat /tmp/_swegemma_junit_"):
                self.last_junit = res.stdout or ""
            return res

        def copy_to(self, a: Any, b: Any, c: Any = None) -> None:
            # The base class already maps any other absolute path (e.g. /var/tmp/x) to
            # <sandbox root>/var/tmp/x, which is where exec's translation points too.
            # Only the image's site-packages needs redirecting, to the venv's.
            if c is not None and isinstance(c, str) and c.startswith(IMAGE_SITE_PACKAGES):
                sid = str(a)
                rel = self._site_packages(sid).relative_to(self._paths(sid)["root"])
                c = "/" + str(rel) + c[len(IMAGE_SITE_PACKAGES):]
            return super().copy_to(a, b, c)

        def extract_archive_to_container(self, container_id: str, archive_path: Path, dst_dir: str = "/workspace") -> bool:
            p = self._paths(container_id)
            if dst_dir in ("/workspace", "/workspace/"):
                dst = p["workspace"]
            elif dst_dir.startswith(IMAGE_SITE_PACKAGES):
                dst = self._site_packages(container_id)
            else:
                dst = p["root"] / dst_dir.lstrip("/")
            dst.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            t0 = time.perf_counter()
            r = subprocess.run(
                ["tar", "--no-same-owner", "--unlink-first", "-xzf", str(archive_path), "-C", str(dst)],
                capture_output=True,
                timeout=3600,  # the Docker path streams over a socket with no command timeout
            )
            self.command_log.append(
                {
                    "step": "extract_archive",
                    "seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - t0, 3),
                    "status": "ok" if r.returncode == 0 else "error",
                    "exit": r.returncode,
                    "timeout": None,
                }
            )
            return r.returncode == 0

    return ContainerManager()

# CELL 10 code
%%writefile g4src/g4bench/power.py
"""How large a difference between two agent configurations the benchmark can resolve.

Setting. Configurations A (baseline) and B (candidate) are each run once on the same
n tasks. For a task drawn from the benchmark's population, the paired outcome falls
in one of four cells; only the two discordant cells matter:

    q_win   B resolves the task and A does not
    q_loss  A resolves the task and B does not

The true gain is delta = q_win - q_loss (in resolution-rate units). Run-to-run
nondeterminism is parameterised by the flip rate f: the probability that a task's
outcome differs between two runs of the *same* configuration. Two identical runs
therefore have q_win = q_loss = f/2. We model a candidate that is better by delta as
keeping the noise-driven losses and adding delta to the wins:

    q_loss = f/2,   q_win = f/2 + delta.

This is the most favourable case for detection: any genuine trade-off (B better on
some tasks, worse on others) adds discordance beyond f and raises every minimum
detectable effect below. The tables are therefore lower bounds on what is needed.

Tests.
* `paired`: the exact McNemar test (two-sided exact binomial test on the discordant
  pairs, p = 1/2), which needs per-task outcomes. Local evaluation provides them.
* `aggregate`: the public leaderboard shows only each submission's resolved count,
  so a comparison can use only W - L (the difference in counts). We give its exact
  power when f is *known*, which the leaderboard never tells you, so it too is a
  lower bound on what the leaderboard needs.

Randomness is over tasks and runs jointly (tasks are a sample from the population
the private split is also drawn from), which is the question a competitor faces:
will the gain hold on 60 other tasks.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import math
from dataclasses import dataclass
from functools import lru_cache


def _log_binom_pmf(k: int, n: int, p: float) -> float:
    if p <= 0.0:
        return 0.0 if k == 0 else -math.inf
    if p >= 1.0:
        return 0.0 if k == n else -math.inf
    return (
        math.lgamma(n + 1) - math.lgamma(k + 1) - math.lgamma(n - k + 1)
        + k * math.log(p) + (n - k) * math.log1p(-p)
    )


def binom_pmf(k: int, n: int, p: float) -> float:
    lp = _log_binom_pmf(k, n, p)
    return 0.0 if lp == -math.inf else math.exp(lp)


def sign_test_p(w: int, d: int) -> float:
    """Exact two-sided binomial test of w successes in d trials against p = 1/2."""
    if d == 0:
        return 1.0
    k = min(w, d - w)
    tail = sum(math.comb(d, i) for i in range(k + 1)) / 2**d
    return min(1.0, 2.0 * tail)


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def mcnemar_critical(d: int, alpha: float = 0.05) -> int | None:
    """Smallest w > d/2 whose two-sided exact p-value is <= alpha; None if none exists."""
    cdf, acc = [], 0.0
    for k in range(d + 1):
        acc += binom_pmf(k, d, 0.5)
        cdf.append(acc)
    for w in range(d // 2 + 1, d + 1):
        if 2 * w > d and 2.0 * cdf[d - w] <= alpha * (1 + 1e-9):
            return w
    return None


def power_paired(n: int, q_win: float, q_loss: float, alpha: float = 0.05) -> float:
    """Exact power of the McNemar test to declare B better (rejections in B's favour only)."""
    q = q_win + q_loss
    if q <= 0.0:
        return 0.0
    r = q_win / q
    total = 0.0
    for d in range(1, n + 1):
        pd = binom_pmf(d, n, q)
        if pd < 1e-15:
            continue
        c = mcnemar_critical(d, alpha)
        if c is None:
            continue
        tail = sum(binom_pmf(w, d, r) for w in range(c, d + 1))
        total += pd * tail
    return total


def _diff_distribution(n: int, q_win: float, q_loss: float) -> dict[int, float]:
    """Exact distribution of W - L over n independent tasks."""
    dist = {0: 1.0}
    q0 = 1.0 - q_win - q_loss
    for _ in range(n):
        nxt: dict[int, float] = {}
        for k, pk in dist.items():
            for step, ps in ((1, q_win), (-1, q_loss), (0, q0)):
                if ps > 0:
                    nxt[k + step] = nxt.get(k + step, 0.0) + pk * ps
        dist = nxt
    return dist


@lru_cache(maxsize=None)
def aggregate_critical(n: int, f: float, alpha: float = 0.05) -> int:
    """Smallest k with P(W - L >= k) <= alpha/2 when A and B are identical (flip rate f)."""
    dist = _diff_distribution(n, f / 2, f / 2)
    for k in range(0, n + 2):
        if sum(p for x, p in dist.items() if x >= k) <= alpha / 2:
            return k
    return n + 1


def power_aggregate(n: int, q_win: float, q_loss: float, f: float, alpha: float = 0.05) -> float:
    k = aggregate_critical(n, round(f, 6), alpha)
    dist = _diff_distribution(n, q_win, q_loss)
    return sum(p for x, p in dist.items() if x >= k)


def mde(
    n: int,
    f: float,
    power: float = 0.80,
    alpha: float = 0.05,
    test: str = "paired",
    tol: float = 1e-4,
) -> float:
    """Minimum detectable gain (resolution-rate units) at the given power."""

    def pw(delta: float) -> float:
        qw, ql = f / 2 + delta, f / 2
        if test == "paired":
            return power_paired(n, qw, ql, alpha)
        return power_aggregate(n, qw, ql, f, alpha)

    lo, hi = 0.0, 1.0 - f
    if pw(hi) < power:
        return math.inf
    while hi - lo > tol:
        mid = (lo + hi) / 2
        if pw(mid) >= power:
            hi = mid
        else:
            lo = mid
    return hi


def mde_asymptotic(n: int, f: float, power: float = 0.80, alpha: float = 0.05) -> float:
    """Connor (1987) normal approximation for McNemar, solved for delta, as a cross-check."""
    from statistics import NormalDist

    za = NormalDist().inv_cdf(1 - alpha / 2)
    zb = NormalDist().inv_cdf(power)
    lo, hi = 1e-6, 1.0 - f
    for _ in range(200):
        mid = (lo + hi) / 2
        d = f + mid  # total discordance q_win + q_loss
        need = (za * math.sqrt(d) + zb * math.sqrt(max(d - mid**2, 1e-12))) ** 2 / mid**2
        if need <= n:
            hi = mid
        else:
            lo = mid
    return hi


def wilson(k: int, n: int, z: float = 1.959963984540054) -> tuple[float, float]:
    """Wilson score interval for a binomial proportion."""
    if n == 0:
        return (0.0, 1.0)
    p = k / n
    den = 1 + z * z / n
    centre = (p + z * z / (2 * n)) / den
    half = z * math.sqrt(p * (1 - p) / n + z * z / (4 * n * n)) / den
    return (max(0.0, centre - half), min(1.0, centre + half))


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Row:
    n: int
    f: float
    test: str
    mde: float


def expected_power(n: int, delta: float, f_grid: list[float], weights: list[float], alpha: float = 0.05) -> float:
    """Power averaged over a prior on the flip rate (discrete grid with weights)."""
    s = sum(weights)
    return sum(w * power_paired(n, f / 2 + delta, f / 2, alpha) for f, w in zip(f_grid, weights)) / s


def mde_under_prior(n: int, f_grid: list[float], weights: list[float], power: float = 0.80, alpha: float = 0.05, tol: float = 1e-4) -> float:
    lo, hi = 0.0, 1.0 - max(f_grid)
    if expected_power(n, hi, f_grid, weights, alpha) < power:
        return math.inf
    while hi - lo > tol:
        mid = (lo + hi) / 2
        if expected_power(n, mid, f_grid, weights, alpha) >= power:
            hi = mid
        else:
            lo = mid
    return hi


def n_for_mde(delta: float, f: float, power: float = 0.80, alpha: float = 0.05, n_max: int = 5000) -> int | None:
    """Smallest n whose paired MDE is at most delta (bisection on n; power is not
    exactly monotone in n for an exact test, so the answer is the first n found by a
    monotone search, reported to the nearest 10)."""
    lo, hi = 1, n_max
    if power_paired(hi, f / 2 + delta, f / 2, alpha) < power:
        return None
    while hi - lo > 10:
        mid = (lo + hi) // 2
        if power_paired(mid, f / 2 + delta, f / 2, alpha) >= power:
            hi = mid
        else:
            lo = mid
    return hi

# CELL 11 code
%%writefile g4src/g4bench/resolution.py
"""An independent reimplementation of the scorer's per-task decision and its aggregate.

Written from a reading of `swegemma.harness.verification.verify_task` (swegemma
0.2.7, the version in Kaggle's wheelhouse dataset), not copied from it. Its purpose
is to be checked against the official code on real runs, so that the paper can state
the rule exactly and a disagreement would surface as a failed check.

The rule, as the shipped code applies it once pytest has run in Container B:

1. pytest must exit 0;
2. a JUnit XML report must exist and parse, record at least one passing test, and
   record no failures and no errors;
3. every test function *added* by `test_patch` (the regex below) must appear in the
   report as passed, and must not also appear failed, errored or skipped (a skipped
   parametrisation of a required test fails the task);
4. pytest's terminal summary must not read as zero passes.

`HARNESS_README.md` section 8.2 states only rule 1 ("resolved if and only if pytest
exits with return code 0"). Rules 2-4 are in the code and not in the document.

Tasks that never reach pytest (the agent produced no patch, the agent patch or the
test patch did not apply, setup raised) are unresolved. The score of a split is
resolved tasks over all tasks in the split; a task with no result counts as
unresolved.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass

_ADDED_TEST_RE = re.compile(r"^\+\s*(?:async\s+)?def\s+(test_[A-Za-z0-9_]+)\s*\(", re.M)
_REMOVED_TEST_RE = re.compile(r"^-\s*(?:async\s+)?def\s+(test_[A-Za-z0-9_]+)\s*\(", re.M)
_HUNK_TEST_RE = re.compile(r"^@@[^@]+@@\s*(?:async\s+)?def\s+(test_[A-Za-z0-9_]+)\b", re.M)
_CONTEXT_TEST_RE = re.compile(r"^ \s*(?:async\s+)?def\s+(test_[A-Za-z0-9_]+)\s*\(", re.M)


def required_tests(test_patch: str) -> list[str]:
    """Test functions the verifier requires to pass, derived from `test_patch` alone.

    If the patch adds any `def test_*`, those are required. Otherwise (the patch only
    edits existing tests) the required set falls back to tests named in hunk headers
    and context lines, minus removed ones and minus prefix collisions.
    """
    if not test_patch:
        return []
    added = _ADDED_TEST_RE.findall(test_patch)
    if added:
        return list(dict.fromkeys(added))
    removed = set(_REMOVED_TEST_RE.findall(test_patch))
    headers = _HUNK_TEST_RE.findall(test_patch)
    context = _CONTEXT_TEST_RE.findall(test_patch)
    context_set = set(context)
    out = []
    for fn in dict.fromkeys(headers + context):
        if fn in removed:
            continue
        if fn not in context_set and (
            any(r.startswith(fn) for r in removed)
            or any(c != fn and c.startswith(fn) for c in context_set)
        ):
            continue
        out.append(fn)
    return out


def _name_matches(node: str, req: str, req_short: str, req_base: str) -> bool:
    if node in (req, req_short, req_base):
        return True
    short = node.rsplit("::", 1)[-1].rsplit(".", 1)[-1]
    if short in (req, req_short, req_base):
        return True
    if short.startswith(req_base + "_") or short.startswith(req_base + "["):
        return True
    if len(req_base) >= 68 and short.startswith(req_base):
        return True
    return short.startswith(req_base) and short[len(req_base):].isdigit()


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class JunitSummary:
    tests: int
    failures: int
    errors: int
    skipped: int

    @property
    def passed(self) -> int:
        return self.tests - self.failures - self.errors - self.skipped


def junit_summary(xml_text: str) -> JunitSummary | None:
    try:
        root = ET.fromstring(xml_text)
    except ET.ParseError:
        return None
    suites = [root] if root.tag == "testsuite" else list(root.findall(".//testsuite"))
    if not suites:
        return None
    get = lambda k: sum(int(s.attrib.get(k, 0)) for s in suites)  # noqa: E731
    return JunitSummary(get("tests"), get("failures"), get("errors"), get("skipped"))


def check_junit(xml_text: str, required: Iterable[str]) -> tuple[bool, str | None]:
    """Rules 2 and 3. Returns (ok, reason-if-not-ok)."""
    if not xml_text or not xml_text.strip():
        return False, "missing or empty JUnit report"
    try:
        root = ET.fromstring(xml_text)
    except ET.ParseError as e:
        return False, f"malformed JUnit report: {e}"
    s = junit_summary(xml_text)
    if s is None:
        return False, "no <testsuite> in JUnit report"
    if s.tests <= 0 or s.passed <= 0:
        return False, f"no passing tests (total={s.tests}, passed={s.passed}, skipped={s.skipped})"
    if s.failures > 0 or s.errors > 0:
        return False, f"failures={s.failures}, errors={s.errors}"
    passed: set[str] = set()
    not_passed: set[str] = set()
    for tc in root.iter("testcase"):
        name = tc.attrib.get("name", "")
        cls = tc.attrib.get("classname", "")
        cands = {name}
        if "[" in name:
            cands.add(name.split("[", 1)[0])
        if cls:
            cands |= {f"{cls}::{name}", f"{cls}.{name}", cls.replace(".", "/") + f".py::{name}"}
        bad = any(tc.find(k) is not None for k in ("failure", "error", "skipped"))
        (not_passed if bad else passed).update(cands)
    for req in required:
        short = req.split("::")[-1]
        base = short.split("[", 1)[0]
        if not any(_name_matches(p, req, short, base) for p in passed):
            return False, f"required test did not pass: {req}"
        if any(_name_matches(p, req, short, base) for p in not_passed):
            return False, f"required test did not pass: {req}"
    return True, None


def stdout_reads_as_no_pass(stdout: str) -> bool:
    s = (stdout or "").lower()
    return bool(
        re.search(r"\b0 passed\b", s)
        or "no tests ran" in s
        or "collected 0 items" in s
        or (s.strip() != "" and "passed" not in s)
    )


def decide(exit_code: int | None, stdout: str, junit_xml: str, test_patch: str) -> tuple[bool, str | None]:
    """The per-task decision once pytest has run, for a real (Docker or subprocess) sandbox."""
    if exit_code != 0:
        return False, f"pytest exit code {exit_code}"
    if junit_xml and junit_xml.strip():
        ok, why = check_junit(junit_xml, required_tests(test_patch))
        if not ok:
            return False, why
        if stdout_reads_as_no_pass(stdout):
            return False, "pytest summary reads as zero passes"
        return True, None
    return False, "missing JUnit report"


def resolution_rate(resolved_by_task: Mapping[str, bool], split: Iterable[str]) -> float:
    """Resolved tasks over all tasks in the split; a task with no result is unresolved."""
    ids = list(split)
    if not ids:
        raise ValueError("empty split")
    return sum(1 for t in ids if resolved_by_task.get(t, False)) / len(ids)

# CELL 12 code
%%writefile g4src/g4bench/symbols.py
"""Map the lines a patch changes to the symbols that enclose them at the base commit.

Used to ask whether the functions a reference fix touches exist as nodes in the
host's code graph. A symbol is named `module.path:Qual.name` where the module path
is the file path with `.py` dropped and `/` turned into `.` (a leading `src/` is
removed); graph node ids are compared on the qualified-name suffix, so the two
conventions need not agree on the module prefix.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import ast
from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Symbol:
    module: str
    qualname: str
    kind: str  # "function" | "class" | "module"
    is_async: bool

    @property
    def dotted(self) -> str:
        return f"{self.module}.{self.qualname}" if self.qualname else self.module


def module_path(path: str) -> str:
    p = path[:-3] if path.endswith(".py") else path
    if p.startswith("src/"):
        p = p[4:]
    if p.endswith("/__init__"):
        p = p[: -len("/__init__")]
    return p.replace("/", ".")


def _spans(tree: ast.AST) -> list[tuple[int, int, str, str, bool, int]]:
    """(start, end, qualname, kind, is_async, depth) for every def/class."""
    out: list[tuple[int, int, str, str, bool, int]] = []

    def visit(node: ast.AST, prefix: str, depth: int) -> None:
        for child in ast.iter_child_nodes(node):
            if isinstance(child, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)):
                q = f"{prefix}.{child.name}" if prefix else child.name
                start = min([child.lineno] + [d.lineno for d in getattr(child, "decorator_list", [])])
                kind = "class" if isinstance(child, ast.ClassDef) else "function"
                out.append((start, child.end_lineno or child.lineno, q, kind, isinstance(child, ast.AsyncFunctionDef), depth))
                visit(child, q, depth + 1)
            else:
                visit(child, prefix, depth)

    visit(tree, "", 0)
    return out


def enclosing_symbols(path: str, source: str, lines: list[int]) -> list[Symbol]:
    """Innermost def/class enclosing each changed line; module level if none.

    Returns an empty list if the source does not parse (the caller records that).
    """
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(source)
    except SyntaxError:
        return []
    spans = _spans(tree)
    mod = module_path(path)
    found: dict[tuple[str, str], Symbol] = {}
    for ln in lines:
        best = None
        for s in spans:
            if s[0] <= ln <= s[1] and (best is None or s[5] > best[5]):
                best = s
        if best is None:
            sym = Symbol(mod, "", "module", False)
        else:
            sym = Symbol(mod, best[2], best[3], best[4])
        found[(sym.module, sym.qualname)] = sym
    return sorted(found.values(), key=lambda s: (s.module, s.qualname))


def in_graph(sym: Symbol, node_ids: set[str], suffix_index: dict[str, set[str]] | None = None) -> bool:
    """Whether a graph has a node for this symbol: the full dotted name, optionally behind a path
    prefix (e.g. `src.`). A bare qualified-name match is not enough: httpx ships twin packages
    (`ahttpx`, `httpx`) whose functions share qualified names."""
    if sym.dotted in node_ids:
        return True
    tail = "." + sym.dotted
    last = sym.dotted.rsplit(".", 1)[-1]
    pool = suffix_index.get(last, ()) if suffix_index is not None else node_ids
    return any(n.endswith(tail) for n in pool)


def build_suffix_index(node_ids: set[str]) -> dict[str, set[str]]:
    idx: dict[str, set[str]] = {}
    for n in node_ids:
        idx.setdefault(n.rsplit(".", 1)[-1], set()).add(n)
    return idx

# CELL 13 code
%%writefile g4src/scripts/audit_tasks.py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Per-task audit of the 129 public practice tasks. CPU only.

    python3 scripts/audit_tasks.py [--tasks PATH] [--listing PATH] [--out results/audit]

Inputs:
  --tasks     tasks.jsonl (Competition Data; default ../data/gemma-4-developer-agent/)
  --listing   the competition's file listing with sizes (default results/audit/file_listing.json,
              fetched with the Kaggle API; --fetch-listing refreshes it)
Optional inputs, merged in when present (each is produced by another script here):
  results/audit/gold_symbols.jsonl       symbols the reference patch changes (verify_scorer.py)
  results/audit/graph_audit.jsonl        graph tool probe and graph content (audit_graphs.py)
  results/verification/runs.jsonl        official-verifier outcomes (verify_scorer.py)

Outputs (in --out):
  tasks_audit.csv / tasks_audit.json     one row per task; no problem statements or patches,
                                         only derived measurements (Competition Data is not
                                         redistributed)
  summary.json / summary.md              the aggregate figures the paper quotes

Every column is defined in COLUMNS below.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import json
import re
import sys
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from pathlib import Path
from statistics import median

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE))

from g4bench import budget, diffs, graphs, resolution  # noqa: E402

COLUMNS = {
    "instance_id": "task id",
    "repo": "source repository",
    "created": "issue/PR creation date (YYYY-MM-DD)",
    "files_changed": "files in the reference patch",
    "code_files_changed": "non-test .py files in the reference patch",
    "lines_added": "added lines in the reference patch",
    "lines_removed": "removed lines in the reference patch",
    "new_files": "files the reference patch creates",
    "touches_docs_src": "reference patch edits FastAPI docs_src/",
    "touches_non_python": "reference patch edits a non-.py file",
    "patch_added_chars": "characters on added lines of the reference patch",
    "patch_added_tokens_approx": "patch_added_chars / 3.5 (approximation; no Gemma tokenizer offline)",
    "test_files": "files in test_patch",
    "required_tests": "test functions the verifier requires (g4bench.resolution.required_tests)",
    "required_async_tests": "required tests defined with async def",
    "ps_chars": "problem statement length",
    "ps_informative_chars": "problem statement length after removing HTML comments and PR-template boilerplate",
    "ps_pr_template": "problem statement is a pull-request template (has '## Pull Request' or '## AI Disclaimer')",
    "ps_has_traceback": "problem statement contains a Python traceback",
    "ps_has_code_block": "problem statement contains a fenced code block",
    "ps_names_changed_path": "problem statement contains the full path of a file the patch changes",
    "ps_names_changed_stem": "problem statement contains a changed file's stem as a word",
    "ps_names_changed_symbol": "problem statement contains a changed function/class name as a word",
    "hints_nonempty": "hints_text is non-empty",
    "graph_class": "A_intact / B_graph_only / C_embeddings_only / D_neither, as the harness reads the files",
    "graph_prompt_block": "harness includes the Code Intelligence Tools block in the prompt",
    "graph_commit_bytes": "size of the commit-named graph (the one the harness reads)",
    "graph_task_bytes": "size of the task-named graph (never read)",
    "embedding_commit_bytes": "size of the commit-named embeddings",
    "embedding_task_bytes": "size of the task-named embeddings",
    "sample_prompt_steers_to_search": "sample system prompt tells the agent to use search_similar_code (no explicit file path in the statement)",
    "search_symbol_status": "search_similar_code(symbol) as shipped: ok / empty / error (audit_graphs.py)",
    "neighbors_status": "get_code_neighbors as shipped: ok / error (audit_graphs.py)",
    "graph_nodes": "nodes in the task's graph (content from either name)",
    "graph_nodes_async_def": "graph nodes whose definition is async def",
    "gold_functions": "functions the reference patch changes that exist at the base commit",
    "gold_functions_async": "of which async",
    "gold_functions_in_graph": "of which present as graph nodes",
    "verify_published_gold": "official verifier resolves the reference patch, published environment",
    "verify_published_empty": "official verifier resolves an empty patch, published environment",
    "verify_published_imported_from": "where the package under test was imported from (published)",
    "verify_published_missing": "missing modules / fixtures seen in pytest output (published)",
    "verify_completed_gold": "same, completed environment",
    "verify_completed_empty": "same, completed environment",
    "verify_noshadow_gold": "same, completed_noshadow environment",
    "verify_noshadow_empty": "same, completed_noshadow environment",
    "verify_pinned_gold": "same, completed_pinned environment (fastapi starlette/pydantic in the commit's declared range)",
    "verify_pinned_empty": "same, completed_pinned environment",
    "verify_pytest_s": "wall seconds of the verification pytest run (gold, best environment available)",
    "verify_max_setup_command_s": "longest single setup command in verification (gold, best environment available)",
}

_COMMENT = re.compile(r"<!--.*?-->", re.S)
_TEMPLATE_LINES = re.compile(
    r"^[ \t]*(## (Pull Request|Description|AI Disclaimer|Checklist)|- \[[ x]\] |Discussion:|<details>|</details>|<summary>.*</summary>|---)[^\n]*$",
    re.M,
)


def informative(ps: str) -> str:
    s = _COMMENT.sub("", ps)
    s = _TEMPLATE_LINES.sub("", s)
    return re.sub(r"\n\s*\n+", "\n", s).strip()


def word_in(text: str, word: str) -> bool:
    return bool(word) and re.search(r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_])" + re.escape(word) + r"(?![A-Za-z0-9_])", text) is not None


def read_jsonl(p: Path) -> list[dict]:
    if not p.exists():
        return []
    return [json.loads(ln) for ln in p.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if ln.strip()]


def fetch_listing(dest: Path) -> None:
    import kaggle  # system Kaggle client

    api = kaggle.api
    api.authenticate()
    rows, tok = [], None
    while True:
        r = api.competition_list_files("gemma-4-developer-agent", page_token=tok, page_size=200)
        rows += [{"name": f.name, "size": int(f.total_bytes or 0)} for f in r.files]
        tok = getattr(r, "next_page_token", None)
        if not tok:
            break
    dest.write_text(json.dumps(sorted(rows, key=lambda x: x["name"]), indent=0) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")


def tool_state(d: dict | None) -> str | None:
    if not d:
        return None
    if d.get("status") != "ok":
        return "error"
    return "empty" if d.get("count") == 0 else "ok"


def audit_task(t: dict, sizes: dict, gold: dict | None, gprobe: dict | None, runs: dict) -> dict:
    fds = diffs.parse_diff(t["patch"])
    tfds = diffs.parse_diff(t["test_patch"])
    code = [f for f in fds if f.path.endswith(".py") and not diffs.is_test_path(f.path)]
    ps = t["problem_statement"]
    info = informative(ps)
    changed_paths = [f.path for f in fds]
    stems = {p.rsplit("/", 1)[-1].rsplit(".", 1)[0] for p in changed_paths}
    stems.discard("__init__")
    sym_names = set()
    if gold and "files" in gold:
        for f in gold["files"]:
            for s in f.get("changed", []):
                if s["qualname"]:
                    sym_names.add(s["qualname"].rsplit(".", 1)[-1])
    else:
        sym_names = {s for _, s, _ in diffs.hunk_context_symbols(fds)}
    req = resolution.required_tests(t["test_patch"])
    async_defs = {n for n, a in diffs.defined_symbols([ln for f in tfds for h in f.hunks for ln in h.added]) if a}
    g = graphs.classify(t, sizes)
    names_path = any(p in ps for p in changed_paths)
    row = {
        "instance_id": t["instance_id"],
        "repo": t["repo"],
        "created": t["created_at"][:10],
        "files_changed": len(fds),
        "code_files_changed": len(code),
        "lines_added": sum(f.added for f in fds),
        "lines_removed": sum(f.removed for f in fds),
        "new_files": sum(1 for f in fds if f.is_new),
        "touches_docs_src": any(p.startswith("docs_src/") for p in changed_paths),
        "touches_non_python": any(not p.endswith(".py") for p in changed_paths),
        "patch_added_chars": sum(len(x) + 1 for f in fds for h in f.hunks for x in h.added),
        "test_files": len(tfds),
        "required_tests": len(req),
        "required_async_tests": sum(1 for r in req if r in async_defs),
        "ps_chars": len(ps),
        "ps_informative_chars": len(info),
        "ps_pr_template": ("## Pull Request" in ps) or ("## AI Disclaimer" in ps),
        "ps_has_traceback": "Traceback (most recent call last)" in ps,
        "ps_has_code_block": "```" in ps,
        "ps_names_changed_path": names_path,
        "ps_names_changed_stem": any(word_in(ps, s) for s in stems),
        "ps_names_changed_symbol": any(word_in(ps, s) for s in sym_names),
        "hints_nonempty": bool((t.get("hints_text") or "").strip()),
        "graph_class": g["class"],
        "graph_prompt_block": g["prompt_block"],
        "graph_commit_bytes": g["sizes"]["graph_commit"],
        "graph_task_bytes": g["sizes"]["graph_task"],
        "embedding_commit_bytes": g["sizes"]["embedding_commit"],
        "embedding_task_bytes": g["sizes"]["embedding_task"],
        "sample_prompt_steers_to_search": not names_path,
    }
    row["patch_added_tokens_approx"] = budget.rough_tokens(row["patch_added_chars"])
    if gprobe and "as_shipped" in gprobe:
        a = gprobe["as_shipped"]
        row["search_symbol_status"] = tool_state(a.get("search_similar_code_symbol"))
        row["neighbors_status"] = tool_state(a.get("get_code_neighbors"))
        c = gprobe.get("content") or {}
        row["graph_nodes"] = c.get("nodes")
        row["graph_nodes_async_def"] = c.get("nodes_async_def")
        if "gold_functions" in c:
            row["gold_functions"] = c["gold_functions_total"]
            row["gold_functions_async"] = sum(1 for s in c["gold_functions"] if s["async"])
            row["gold_functions_in_graph"] = c["gold_functions_in_graph"]
    for env, tag in (("published", "published"), ("completed", "completed"), ("completed_noshadow", "noshadow"),
                     ("completed_pinned", "pinned")):
        gr = runs.get((t["instance_id"], env, "gold"))
        er = runs.get((t["instance_id"], env, "empty"))
        if gr is not None:
            row[f"verify_{tag}_gold"] = gr["resolved"]
        if er is not None:
            row[f"verify_{tag}_empty"] = er["resolved"]
        if env == "published" and gr is not None:
            row["verify_published_imported_from"] = gr.get("under_test_imported_from")
            miss = gr.get("missing_modules", []) + [f"fixture:{x}" for x in gr.get("missing_fixtures", [])]
            row["verify_published_missing"] = ";".join(miss)
    for env in ("completed_pinned", "completed_noshadow", "completed", "published"):
        gr = runs.get((t["instance_id"], env, "gold"))
        if gr is not None and gr.get("pytest_ran"):
            row["verify_pytest_s"] = gr.get("pytest_s")
            row["verify_max_setup_command_s"] = gr.get("max_setup_command_s")
            break
    return {k: row.get(k) for k in COLUMNS}


def pct(a: int, b: int) -> str:
    return f"{a}/{b} ({100 * a / b:.0f}%)" if b else "0/0"


def summarize(rows: list[dict], tasks: list[dict]) -> dict:
    n = len(rows)
    by_repo = Counter(r["repo"] for r in rows)
    shares = [c / n for c in by_repo.values()]
    s: dict = {"tasks": n, "by_repo": dict(by_repo), "effective_number_of_repos": round(1 / sum(x * x for x in shares), 2),
               "largest_repo_share": round(max(shares), 3)}
    s["created_by_year"] = dict(sorted(Counter(r["created"][:4] for r in rows).items()))
    lc = sorted(r["lines_added"] + r["lines_removed"] for r in rows)
    s["lines_changed"] = {"median": median(lc), "p25": lc[len(lc) // 4], "p75": lc[(3 * len(lc)) // 4], "max": lc[-1],
                          "le_10": sum(1 for x in lc if x <= 10), "gt_100": sum(1 for x in lc if x > 100)}
    s["files_changed"] = {"median": median(r["files_changed"] for r in rows), "single_file": sum(1 for r in rows if r["files_changed"] == 1),
                          "three_or_more": sum(1 for r in rows if r["files_changed"] >= 3), "max": max(r["files_changed"] for r in rows)}
    for k in ("new_files",):
        s["tasks_creating_files"] = sum(1 for r in rows if r[k] > 0)
    for k in ("touches_docs_src", "touches_non_python", "ps_pr_template", "ps_has_traceback", "ps_has_code_block",
              "ps_names_changed_path", "ps_names_changed_stem", "ps_names_changed_symbol", "hints_nonempty",
              "graph_prompt_block", "sample_prompt_steers_to_search"):
        s[k] = sum(1 for r in rows if r[k])
    info = sorted(r["ps_informative_chars"] for r in rows)
    s["ps_informative_chars"] = {"median": median(info), "min": info[0], "under_200": sum(1 for x in info if x < 200)}
    s["required_tests"] = {"median": median(r["required_tests"] for r in rows), "zero": sum(1 for r in rows if r["required_tests"] == 0),
                           "with_async": sum(1 for r in rows if r["required_async_tests"])}
    # graph trap
    gc = defaultdict(Counter)
    for r in rows:
        gc[r["graph_class"]][r["repo"]] += 1
    s["graph_class_by_repo"] = {k: dict(v) for k, v in sorted(gc.items())}
    s["graph_class"] = {k: sum(v.values()) for k, v in sorted(gc.items())}
    s["graph_commit_empty"] = sum(1 for r in rows if not (r["graph_commit_bytes"] or 0) > 100)
    s["embedding_commit_empty"] = sum(1 for r in rows if not (r["embedding_commit_bytes"] or 0) > 100)
    steer = [r for r in rows if r["sample_prompt_steers_to_search"]]
    s["steered_to_search_and_graph_unusable"] = sum(1 for r in steer if r["graph_class"] in ("C_embeddings_only", "D_neither"))
    s["steered_to_search_and_search_empty_or_error"] = sum(1 for r in steer if r["graph_class"] != "A_intact")
    probed = [r for r in rows if r.get("search_symbol_status") is not None]
    if probed:
        s["probe"] = {
            "tasks_probed": len(probed),
            "search_symbol": dict(Counter(r["search_symbol_status"] for r in probed)),
            "neighbors": dict(Counter(r["neighbors_status"] for r in probed)),
        }
    gf = [r for r in rows if r.get("gold_functions") is not None]
    if gf:
        s["gold_function_coverage"] = {
            "tasks": len(gf),
            "functions": sum(r["gold_functions"] for r in gf),
            "in_graph": sum(r["gold_functions_in_graph"] for r in gf),
            "async": sum(r["gold_functions_async"] for r in gf),
            "tasks_with_a_missing_gold_function": sum(1 for r in gf if r["gold_functions_in_graph"] < r["gold_functions"]),
            "graph_nodes_async_def_total": sum(r.get("graph_nodes_async_def") or 0 for r in gf),
        }
    # eval_config trap (per-task where measurable)
    e = budget.effective(budget.SAMPLE_EVAL_CONFIG)
    s["sample_eval_config"] = {
        "effective": e.__dict__,
        "budget_warning_ever_shown": budget.warning_ever_shown(e.tool_calls),
        "break_even_decode_tokens_per_s_for_one_full_thinking_turn": round(budget.break_even_decode_rate(4096, 60.0), 1),
        "tasks_patch_over_60s_at_20_tok_s": sum(1 for r in rows if r["patch_added_tokens_approx"] > 20 * 60),
        "tasks_patch_over_60s_at_50_tok_s": sum(1 for r in rows if r["patch_added_tokens_approx"] > 50 * 60),
    }
    timed = [r for r in rows if r.get("verify_max_setup_command_s") is not None]
    if timed:
        s["sample_eval_config"]["tasks_timed"] = len(timed)
        s["sample_eval_config"]["setup_command_over_60s"] = sorted(r["instance_id"] for r in timed if r["verify_max_setup_command_s"] > 60)
        s["sample_eval_config"]["verify_pytest_over_60s"] = sorted(r["instance_id"] for r in timed if (r["verify_pytest_s"] or 0) > 60)
        s["sample_eval_config"]["max_setup_command_s"] = max(r["verify_max_setup_command_s"] for r in timed)
        s["sample_eval_config"]["max_verify_pytest_s"] = max((r["verify_pytest_s"] or 0) for r in timed)
    for tag in ("published", "completed", "noshadow", "pinned"):
        gk, ek = f"verify_{tag}_gold", f"verify_{tag}_empty"
        g_ = [r for r in rows if r.get(gk) is not None]
        e_ = [r for r in rows if r.get(ek) is not None]
        if g_ or e_:
            per = defaultdict(Counter)
            for r in g_:
                per[r["repo"]]["gold_run"] += 1
                per[r["repo"]]["gold_resolved"] += int(r[gk])
            for r in e_:
                per[r["repo"]]["empty_run"] += 1
                per[r["repo"]]["empty_resolved"] += int(r[ek])
            s[f"verification_{tag}"] = {
                "gold_run": len(g_), "gold_resolved": sum(1 for r in g_ if r[gk]),
                "empty_run": len(e_), "empty_resolved": sum(1 for r in e_ if r[ek]),
                "valid_pairs": sum(1 for r in rows if r.get(gk) is True and r.get(ek) is False),
                "by_repo": {k: dict(v) for k, v in sorted(per.items())},
            }
    return s


def to_markdown(s: dict) -> str:
    n = s["tasks"]
    L = ["# Audit of the public practice tasks", "", f"Tasks: {n}. Repositories: " +
         ", ".join(f"{k} {v}" for k, v in sorted(s["by_repo"].items(), key=lambda kv: -kv[1])) +
         f". Effective number of repositories (inverse Herfindahl): {s['effective_number_of_repos']}.", ""]
    L.append(f"- Created by year: {s['created_by_year']}")
    lc = s["lines_changed"]
    L.append(f"- Lines changed by the reference patch: median {lc['median']} (quartiles {lc['p25']}/{lc['p75']}), max {lc['max']}; "
             f"{lc['le_10']} tasks change 10 or fewer, {lc['gt_100']} change more than 100")
    fc = s["files_changed"]
    L.append(f"- Files changed: median {fc['median']}; {fc['single_file']} single-file; {fc['three_or_more']} touch 3 or more; max {fc['max']}; "
             f"{s['tasks_creating_files']} create a file; {s['touches_docs_src']} edit docs_src/")
    L.append(f"- Problem statements: {s['ps_pr_template']} are PR templates; informative length median {s['ps_informative_chars']['median']} chars, "
             f"{s['ps_informative_chars']['under_200']} under 200; {s['ps_has_traceback']} contain a traceback; "
             f"{s['ps_names_changed_path']} name a changed file's full path, {s['ps_names_changed_stem']} its stem, "
             f"{s['ps_names_changed_symbol']} a changed function or class; {s['hints_nonempty']} have hints")
    rt = s["required_tests"]
    L.append(f"- Required tests per task: median {rt['median']}; {rt['zero']} tasks with none derivable; {rt['with_async']} with an async required test")
    L += ["", "## Code-graph files as the harness reads them", ""]
    L.append(f"- Commit-named graph empty: {s['graph_commit_empty']}/{n}; commit-named embeddings empty: {s['embedding_commit_empty']}/{n}")
    L.append(f"- Prompt block shown: {s['graph_prompt_block']}/{n}")
    L.append("- Classes: " + ", ".join(f"{k} {v}" for k, v in s["graph_class"].items()))
    for k, v in s["graph_class_by_repo"].items():
        L.append(f"  - {k}: {v}")
    L.append(f"- Sample prompt steers to search_similar_code (no explicit path): {s['sample_prompt_steers_to_search']}/{n}; "
             f"of those, graph unusable (classes C, D): {s['steered_to_search_and_graph_unusable']}; "
             f"search empty or failing (classes B, C, D): {s['steered_to_search_and_search_empty_or_error']}")
    if "probe" in s:
        L.append(f"- Harness tool probe on {s['probe']['tasks_probed']} tasks: search_similar_code(symbol) {s['probe']['search_symbol']}; "
                 f"get_code_neighbors {s['probe']['neighbors']}")
    if "gold_function_coverage" in s:
        g = s["gold_function_coverage"]
        L.append(f"- Functions the reference patches change: {g['functions']} over {g['tasks']} tasks; {g['in_graph']} are graph nodes; "
                 f"{g['async']} are async; {g['tasks_with_a_missing_gold_function']} tasks have at least one changed function absent")
    L += ["", "## The sample eval_config.yaml", ""]
    e = s["sample_eval_config"]
    L.append(f"- Effective (if applied as EvalConfig keyword arguments): {e['effective']}; budget warning ever shown: {e['budget_warning_ever_shown']}")
    L.append(f"- One turn that uses the full 4,096-token thinking budget exceeds the minute below {e['break_even_decode_tokens_per_s_for_one_full_thinking_turn']} tokens/s")
    if "tasks_timed" in e:
        L.append(f"- Verification timings on {e['tasks_timed']} tasks (this machine): longest setup command {e['max_setup_command_s']} s, "
                 f"longest pytest {e['max_verify_pytest_s']} s; setup over 60 s: {len(e['setup_command_over_60s'])}; pytest over 60 s: {len(e['verify_pytest_over_60s'])}")
    for tag in ("published", "completed", "noshadow", "pinned"):
        k = f"verification_{tag}"
        if k in s:
            v = s[k]
            L += ["", f"## Official verifier, {tag} environment", "",
                  f"- Gold resolved {v['gold_resolved']}/{v['gold_run']}; empty resolved {v['empty_resolved']}/{v['empty_run']}; "
                  f"valid (gold passes, empty fails) {v['valid_pairs']}"]
            for repo, c in v["by_repo"].items():
                L.append(f"  - {repo}: {dict(c)}")
    return "\n".join(L) + "\n"


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument("--tasks", default=str(HERE.parent / "data" / "gemma-4-developer-agent" / "tasks.jsonl"))
    ap.add_argument("--listing", default=str(HERE / "results" / "audit" / "file_listing.json"))
    ap.add_argument("--fetch-listing", action="store_true")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=str(HERE / "results" / "audit"))
    args = ap.parse_args(argv)
    out = Path(args.out)
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    if args.fetch_listing:
        fetch_listing(Path(args.listing))
    tasks = read_jsonl(Path(args.tasks))
    sizes = graphs.load_listing(Path(args.listing))
    gold = {r["instance_id"]: r for r in read_jsonl(out / "gold_symbols.jsonl")}
    gprobe = {r["instance_id"]: r for r in read_jsonl(out / "graph_audit.jsonl")}
    runs = {}
    for r in read_jsonl(HERE / "results" / "verification" / "runs.jsonl"):
        runs[(r["instance_id"], r.get("env", "published"), r["arm"])] = r
    rows = [audit_task(t, sizes, gold.get(t["instance_id"]), gprobe.get(t["instance_id"]), runs) for t in tasks]
    with (out / "tasks_audit.csv").open("w", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        w = csv.DictWriter(fh, fieldnames=list(COLUMNS))
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(rows)
    (out / "tasks_audit.json").write_text(json.dumps({"columns": COLUMNS, "rows": rows}, indent=1) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    s = summarize(rows, tasks)
    (out / "summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(s, indent=1, sort_keys=True) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    md = to_markdown(s)
    (out / "summary.md").write_text(md, encoding="utf-8")
    print(md)
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

# CELL 14 code
%%writefile g4src/scripts/power_table.py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Write the benchmark's resolution tables to results/power/.

    python3 scripts/power_table.py [--out results/power]

Outputs power_table.json (every number), power_table.csv (the MDE grid) and
power_table.md (the tables the paper quotes). Model and tests: g4bench/power.py.
Runs in a few seconds on CPU.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import json
import sys
from pathlib import Path

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(HERE))

from g4bench import power as P  # noqa: E402

NS = (40, 60, 120, 129)
FLIPS = (0.0, 0.025, 0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20)
# The flip rate has not been measured on this benchmark. Our recorded prediction
# (docs/EXPERIMENTS.md, 2026-09-24 section 7) is 10%, band 5-20%. The prior below is
# uniform on that band, on a 1-point grid.
PRIOR_GRID = [round(0.05 + 0.01 * i, 2) for i in range(16)]
PRIOR_W = [1.0] * len(PRIOR_GRID)


def pts(x: float) -> float | None:
    return None if x == float("inf") else round(100 * x, 1)


def build() -> dict:
    grid = []
    for n in NS:
        for f in FLIPS:
            row = {
                "n": n,
                "flip_rate": f,
                "mde_paired_points": pts(P.mde(n, f, test="paired")),
                "mde_paired_asymptotic_points": pts(P.mde_asymptotic(n, f)) if f > 0 else pts(P.mde_asymptotic(n, 1e-9)),
                "mde_aggregate_known_f_points": pts(P.mde(n, f, test="aggregate")) if f > 0 else None,
            }
            grid.append(row)
    prior = {n: pts(P.mde_under_prior(n, PRIOR_GRID, PRIOR_W)) for n in NS}
    needed = []
    for delta in (0.03, 0.05, 0.10):
        for f in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20):
            needed.append({"gain_points": round(100 * delta), "flip_rate": f, "tasks_needed": P.n_for_mde(delta, f)})
    precision = []
    for n in (60, 129):
        for p in (0.06, 0.12, 0.20, 0.30):
            k = round(p * n)
            lo, hi = P.wilson(k, n)
            precision.append({"n": n, "score": round(k / n, 3), "resolved": k, "wilson95": [round(100 * lo, 1), round(100 * hi, 1)],
                              "width_points": round(100 * (hi - lo), 1)})
    flip_measure = []
    for n in (40, 60, 129):
        for f in (0.05, 0.10, 0.20):
            k = round(f * n)
            lo, hi = P.wilson(k, n)
            flip_measure.append({"n": n, "true_flip_rate": f, "flips": k, "wilson95": [round(100 * lo, 1), round(100 * hi, 1)]})
    return {
        "model": "q_loss = f/2, q_win = f/2 + gain; alpha = 0.05 two-sided; power 0.80; see g4bench/power.py",
        "grid": grid,
        "mde_under_prior_uniform_5_to_20pct": prior,
        "prior_grid": PRIOR_GRID,
        "tasks_needed_paired": needed,
        "single_score_precision": precision,
        "flip_rate_from_one_pair_of_runs": flip_measure,
        "one_task_points": {n: round(100 / n, 2) for n in NS},
    }


def to_markdown(d: dict) -> str:
    L = ["# What the benchmark can resolve", "",
         "Minimum detectable gain (percentage points of resolution rate) at 80% power, two-sided alpha 0.05.",
         "Model: q_loss = f/2, q_win = f/2 + gain, where f is the flip rate between two identical runs.",
         "Paired = exact McNemar on per-task outcomes (local evaluation). Aggregate = exact test on the",
         "difference in resolved counts with f known (the leaderboard shows only counts; f is never shown,",
         "so this column is a best case for the leaderboard).", ""]
    L.append("| Tasks | Flip rate f | Paired (exact) | Paired (asymptotic) | Aggregate, f known |")
    L.append("|---|---|---|---|---|")
    for r in d["grid"]:
        agg = "-" if r["mde_aggregate_known_f_points"] is None else f"{r['mde_aggregate_known_f_points']}"
        L.append(f"| {r['n']} | {100 * r['flip_rate']:g}% | {r['mde_paired_points']} | {r['mde_paired_asymptotic_points']} | {agg} |")
    L += ["", "Paired MDE averaged over a uniform prior on f in [5%, 20%] (power averaged over the prior):", ""]
    L.append("| Tasks | MDE (points) |")
    L.append("|---|---|")
    for n, v in d["mde_under_prior_uniform_5_to_20pct"].items():
        L.append(f"| {n} | {v} |")
    L += ["", "Tasks needed (paired, 80% power) for a given true gain:", "", "| Gain | f = 5% | f = 10% | f = 20% |", "|---|---|---|---|"]
    by = {}
    for r in d["tasks_needed_paired"]:
        by.setdefault(r["gain_points"], {})[r["flip_rate"]] = r["tasks_needed"]
    for g, row in by.items():
        L.append(f"| +{g} | {row[0.05]} | {row[0.10]} | {row[0.20]} |")
    L += ["", "Precision of a single score (Wilson 95% interval):", "", "| Tasks | Score | Interval | Width |", "|---|---|---|---|"]
    for r in d["single_score_precision"]:
        L.append(f"| {r['n']} | {100 * r['score']:.1f}% | {r['wilson95'][0]}-{r['wilson95'][1]}% | {r['width_points']} |")
    L += ["", "What one pair of identical runs tells you about f (Wilson 95% interval at the expected flip count):", "",
          "| Tasks | True f | Flips | Interval |", "|---|---|---|---|"]
    for r in d["flip_rate_from_one_pair_of_runs"]:
        L.append(f"| {r['n']} | {100 * r['true_flip_rate']:g}% | {r['flips']} | {r['wilson95'][0]}-{r['wilson95'][1]}% |")
    return "\n".join(L) + "\n"


def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=str(HERE / "results" / "power"))
    args = ap.parse_args(argv)
    out = Path(args.out)
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    d = build()
    (out / "power_table.json").write_text(json.dumps(d, indent=1, sort_keys=True) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    with (out / "power_table.csv").open("w", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        w = csv.DictWriter(fh, fieldnames=list(d["grid"][0].keys()))
        w.writeheader()
        w.writerows(d["grid"])
    (out / "power_table.md").write_text(to_markdown(d), encoding="utf-8")
    print(to_markdown(d))
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

# CELL 15 code
%%writefile g4src/scripts/audit_graphs.py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Per-task audit of the code graphs and embeddings, and of what the graph tools return.

For each public task this fetches the task's four graph/embedding files (task-named
and commit-named; one side of each hard-linked pair is empty on Kaggle), lays them
out under their real names, and then:

1. asks the harness itself what an agent would get: whether `build_agent_prompt`
   includes the "Code Intelligence Tools" block, and what `get_code_neighbors`,
   `get_code_subgraph` and `search_similar_code` return (the latter both with a
   symbol name, as HARNESS_README.md recommends, and with a phrase from the issue,
   as the sample system prompt instructs), with the tool-call count they charge;
2. repeats (1) after repairing the layout (content copied to both names), to show
